    {
      "path": "input-gap-repairs/owned-safe-cleanup/outputs/source-reads/references/workflow-ownership.md",
      "bytes": 1265,
      "sha256": "50644b3a9a3dfd5651382eaca1d3a2728af0b3157d6d66b730930370a64bb03c",
      "owner": "local-013-input-gap-repair-owned-safe-cleanup",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "# Workflow ownership\n\nAn explicit pstack invocation gives pstack ownership of that workflow. An explicit Superpowers invocation gives Superpowers ownership. Do not run both controllers over the same assignment or let one silently replace the other's plan, review, or integration decisions.\n\nFor an unqualified request, D1 retains the already active workflow. With no active workflow, use host defaults and do not activate either orchestration mode automatically. If both pstack and Superpowers are explicitly requested, clarify ownership before launching workers or changing artifacts. Preserve the active controller across follow-ups unless the user changes ownership. A helper skill can advise the owner without becoming a second controller.\n\nThe owner assigns one coordinator. Workers produce isolated artifacts and evidence; the coordinator reviews and integrates them. Shared inventory and registry writes belong to the coordinator. Worker cancellation, stale results or unavailable tools do not transfer that ownership automatically.\n\nHost permissions and user authorization constrain every owner. Local structural tests, documented APIs and declared cases provide no host pass. Explicit reduced or blocked results remain visible beside the full expectation.\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "input-gap-repairs/owned-safe-cleanup/outputs/source-reads/references/agent-prompts/poteto-agent.md",
      "bytes": 1221,
      "sha256": "9839239c6ebc72de8165f8a6fa4a6326fea7293e15a47d24f98396df2a61b3fb",
      "owner": "local-013-input-gap-repair-owned-safe-cleanup",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "# Poteto agent prompt\n\nUse this role for `/poteto-mode` and requests for poteto's style. Supply these instructions to a fresh independent worker for each new task when the session exposes and permits that operation. Resume a worker only in the strict cases named by poteto-mode's Subagents section. Generic worker routing is acceptable only when it carries this entire prompt and the required mode read; otherwise it skips the style contract.\n\nBefore any work, read [poteto-mode's SKILL.md](../../skills/poteto-mode/SKILL.md) in full, including its inline Principles index. Navigate to the leaf `principle-*` skill whenever you apply that principle. If the mode or an applicable leaf is unavailable, report that missing dependency and stop dependent work. Do not reconstruct the mode from a summary.\n\nFollow [model roles](../model-roles.md) and [session capabilities](../host-capabilities.md). This prompt does not register an agent or promise background execution. Record requested and observed models separately. Preserve user authorization and host permissions throughout the mode. An authorized sequential fallback uses the same full read and discloses reduced capability; it does not count as an independent worker.\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "input-gap-repairs/owned-safe-cleanup/outputs/source-reads/references/host-capabilities.md",
      "bytes": 7155,
      "sha256": "f0f09ce28b6feca4d72694d5687204dc074aa58dade9a0ab59656da3bfa1bd20",
      "owner": "local-013-input-gap-repair-owned-safe-cleanup",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "# Discover capabilities in the current session\n\nRead the tools exposed to this session before assigning work. Cached official-subagents.txt is documentation context; it proves no operation succeeded. Local agent configuration is not portable plugin configuration.\n\nRecord each binding as `{operation, tool, exposed, succeeded, evidence, limits}`. `tool` is the exact exposed identifier or null when absent. `succeeded` is true only after observing a result. Keep failed and unavailable bindings. Successful bindings become `successfulOperations` in host evidence, with a root-relative evidence file. The operation labels below describe purposes, never callable APIs.\n\n| Operation | Discovery and observation required |\n| --- | --- |\n| spawn | Find an exposed independent-worker operation; retain returned worker identity and exact assigned revision. |\n| follow-up | Find the operation that sends input to that worker; observe acceptance by the same identity. |\n| wait | Find the operation that waits for worker state; retain terminal or interrupted state. |\n| inspect-result | Read the worker result and its referenced artifacts; a wait notification alone is insufficient. |\n| stop | Observe cancellation or termination through the exposed operation. |\n| read | Observe the requested file content through the available file tool. |\n| isolated-write | Observe a write within the worker's assigned path and permissions. |\n| history | Observe available prior turns or records; declare gaps explicitly. |\n| execution | Observe command execution with exit status and output. |\n| persistence | Write and read an authorized durable record; a claimed save is insufficient. |\n| external-write | Record explicit authorization and action identity; inspect the result before replay. |\n| resolve-ownership | Observe distinct owned paths before contested writes. |\n| inspect-terminal | Observe the worker's terminal state after stop. |\n| quarantine-artifacts | Observe partial artifacts withheld from integration. |\n| inspect-prior-action | Read the interrupted action identity and prior outcome. |\n| inspect-destination | Observe the destination state for that action identity. |\n| safe-recovery | Observe explicitly authorized idempotent recovery after identity and destination inspection. |\n\nNo default API names are assigned here. Tool names differ by host and session. An exposed tool may still be denied or fail. Record requested and actual models only when observed; otherwise use `unknown`. A requested model never proves the actual model.\n\nA denied or unavailable operation blocks the branch that requires it. Sequential substitution for required independent work is reduced capability and uses `reduced-pass`; ordinary direct completion can fully pass. Missing persistence limits session pickup even when current work succeeds. Preserve user authorization and host permission boundaries through every fallback.\n\nWorker passes require observed spawn and result inspection. Each registry entry declares `requiredObservations` for `pass` and `reduced-pass`; tool observations need exposed bindings and existing evidence artifacts; native text observations use the separate reviewed native record below. Full cancellation requires stop, terminal inspection and artifact quarantine. Full persistence requires durable write and readback. Full interrupted-action recovery requires prior identity inspection, destination inspection, safe recovery, explicit external permission and a recorded prior action identity even when no external-write observation is listed.\n\nPermission checks follow performed operations. Isolated writes and persistence require granted writes. External writes and safe recovery require granted external permission and prior identity. Authorized read-only reductions may retain denied writes; their limits must preserve denied or unavailable permissions. Required observations remain applicable to those reductions.\n\nHost evidence is stored as object or array JSON files under `docs/parity/evidence/`. Required fields are defined by `docs/parity/evidence.schema.json`. Artifacts and operation evidence must be existing files inside the tested root. Test fixtures belong in `tests/` and prove validator behavior only.\n\nRun `python3 tools/validate_evidence.py --root . --commit <tested-package-commit>` through Bun, for example `bun -e 'const p=Bun.spawnSync([\"python3\",\"-B\",\"tools/validate_evidence.py\",\"--root\",\".\",\"--commit\",process.argv[1]],{env:{...process.env,PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE:\"1\"}});process.stdout.write(p.stdout);process.stderr.write(p.stderr);process.exit(p.exitCode)' <tested-package-commit>`. It reads every JSON record below the evidence directory, prints each error, and exits 1 for invalid records or 0 for valid records. An empty directory has no invalid records and supplies no behavioral evidence.\n\nThe required registry file in `docs/parity/case-registry.json` declares applicability. Missing or non-file registry, unknown case, structural-only entry or unlisted host rejects behavioral validation. Maintenance fixtures use a temporary copy of this registry; they cannot bypass applicability. Required hosts must receive observed evidence or explicit blocked records before release claims. Structural checks have no required host and cannot populate host status. Later producers propose task-owned entries; only the coordinator integrates them. Source paths select callers through the task 3 case contract. Case definitions alone leave host statuses `not-run`.\n\n## Direct completion and recorded observations\n\n`direct` means the task is completed in the current context. Restatement, supplied-text writing and complete installed discovery can pass directly. `sequential` is reserved for replacing a required independent method with sequential work and remains reduced. A text response has no invented callable tool. Record supplied input, the actual response and qualitative outcome review as `nativeObservations`, each with its existing listed evidence artifact and named reviewer. Their labels are `supplied-input`, `response` and `review-outcome`. The reviewer assesses the case's expected outcome; validation checks evidence relationships and does not decide prose quality.\n\nEach registry entry declares allowed execution modes and required permissions for each status. A grant is required for the performed action: worker spawn, command execution and stop need execution; isolated output or persistence needs writes; external actions need their separate permission and prior identity. Read-only discovery does not inherit an execution grant requirement. Tool observations still name an actually exposed tool and existing evidence. Native observations cannot claim tool discovery, worker identity or saved writes.\n\nFull Arena and Swarm results additionally provide `inputRevision` and a listed `methodReceipt` artifact. An exposed `inspect-method` operation must reference that receipt. Assignment operations name `assignmentId` and the corresponding inspected output. See the [structured method contract](orchestration-contract.md). All positive maintenance fixtures are synthetic; none is an observed host result.\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "input-gap-repairs/owned-safe-cleanup/outputs/source-reads/references/model-roles.md",
      "bytes": 2869,
      "sha256": "c86696b6c568b7db80a7e339856986e64482ce6a418bb91946ce3a3b772f79a1",
      "owner": "local-013-input-gap-repair-owned-safe-cleanup",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "# Model selection for portable roles\n\nUse [session capabilities](host-capabilities.md) before assigning a role. These [poteto](agent-prompts/poteto-agent.md) and [comment reviewer](agent-prompts/comment-sicko.md) prompts are instructions supplied to an exposed worker operation. Reading them does not register a custom agent or authorize delegation.\n\n| Role | Default requested model | Observed model | Supported effort | Fallback disclosure |\n| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |\n| poteto-agent | inherit-parent | unknown until observed | inherit-parent unless a supported explicit effort is verified | Record rejected selection, replacement request, observed result and limits. |\n| comment-sicko | inherit-parent | unknown until observed | inherit-parent unless a supported explicit effort is verified | Record rejected selection, replacement request, observed result and limits. |\n\nFor each assignment, record requested model and effort, observed model and effort, supported effort values and their source, worker identity, and fallback disclosure. Use `unknown` for information the operation does not expose. `inherit-parent` records a selection policy, not an observed model name. Do not silently convert a requested model into an actual model.\n\nSelect an explicit model only from current exposed tool documentation or observed enumeration results. If enumeration is missing, disclose that limit and retain `inherit-parent`. Documentation examples are not a list of available models. Use only an effort supported for the selected model by the current operation; do not carry an incompatible inherited effort into an explicit selection.\n\nIf a selected model or effort is rejected, retain the rejection. Use an authorized inherited or supported alternative only after disclosing the changed selection. If the task requires that exact selection, stop and record blocked. A fallback never proves the original selection succeeded.\n\nIdentical observed models mean no model diversity. Different roles can supply distinct instructions on the same model. Unknown observed models leave diversity unverified, even when requested model names differ. Different observed models support a model-diversity statement only for those observed assignments. Worker independence still requires successful spawn and result inspection.\n\nWhen workers are unavailable, apply an authorized role sequentially and label reduced capability. Preserve scope and permission boundaries. Do not claim independent review, background execution, or full worker support from sequential role switching.\n\nThe cached documentation from https://learn.chatgpt.com/docs/agent-configuration/subagents describes inheritance and optional local agent configuration. It is context only. This package writes no local custom-agent configuration and claims no current host model or worker support from that documentation.\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "input-gap-repairs/owned-safe-cleanup/outputs/source-reads/references/orchestration-contract.md",
      "bytes": 6273,
      "sha256": "5dd740ad66d992df252754d189d0dc207900023372392a0db4309b85eb5e4ec5",
      "owner": "local-013-input-gap-repair-owned-safe-cleanup",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "# Assignment and integration contract\n\nBefore dispatch, the coordinator creates an immutable assignment with `id`, `scope`, `inputRevision`, `outputOwner`, `isolatedOutputPath`, `role`, `retryCount`, `terminalStatus`, and `evidenceLinks`. Use the full Git SHA for inputRevision, an exact owned path, and an explicit role. Start retryCount at 0 and terminalStatus at `pending`. Preserve every attempt as an append-only event under the same assignment ID; update status through events, never rewrite the original scope, revision, owner or path. A changed scope or revision requires a new assignment ID that links its predecessor.\n\nThe coordinator checks authorization and observed host bindings before dispatch. One path has one writer. Reject overlapping paths before work starts; assign distinct paths instead. Workers write only their isolated output and report results. Only the coordinator integrates shared files, inventory, registry, or other workers' output.\n\nAccept a result only when its assignment identity and input SHA match, its artifacts exist, and required method evidence was observed. Reject stale revisions. Permit at most one retry for missing required SHA or method evidence, after inspecting the prior attempt and confirming the same permissions still apply. A retry count of 1 exhausts this allowance. Denied permissions, unknown action outcomes, and missing artifacts never become success by retry policy.\n\nTerminal states are `completed`, `blocked`, `failed`, and `cancelled`. A completion event links the exact result and evidence. Stop a cancelled worker through the exposed stop binding when available, then inspect its terminal state. Quarantine any partial artifacts; cancellation does not authorize their integration. If stop cannot be observed, record that limit and do not claim termination. Report interrupted execution explicitly.\n\nBefore an external action, record explicit permission, a stable action identity, the intended destination and the last observed outcome. After interruption, inspect whether that identity already completed. Retry only with authorization, an observed safe replay decision and an idempotency mechanism appropriate to the destination. If the outcome is unknown, block replay. Do not infer permission from upstream autonomy instructions.\n\nHistory and persistence have separate bindings. Keep assignment events and result links in an authorized durable location and verify readback. If unavailable, declare the gap and offer an authorized local handoff as reduced capability; never claim unattended recovery or cross-session continuity. All host statuses remain `not-run` until actual observations exist.\n\n## Structured Arena and Swarm receipts\n\nA method receipt is JSON with schemaVersion 1, kind `arena` or `swarm`, inputRevision, assignments, reviewArtifact, reviewedBy and declaredOrder. The inputRevision is the actual task repository revision, distinct from the packageCommit identifying these instructions. It must match the evidence record. The declared order precedes every dispatch. reviewArtifact records the qualitative outcome review and reviewedBy identifies its reviewer. All referenced artifacts must exist and be listed in the evidence record.\n\nEach assignment records id, workerId, inputRevision, outputOwner, outputPath, role, status, startedOrder, completedOrder, requestedModel, actualModel, retryCount, method, taskArtifact, rubricProvided, resultRevision, resultMethod and outputSha256. Result revision and method must match the assignment, and the inspected output bytes must match their digest. Worker and assignment identities are distinct. outputOwner equals workerId; output directories do not overlap. Integer order values record the observed dispatch and completion sequence, not estimated time. The method has positive sampleCount, sampleDefinition and order. Missing model observations use unknown. The validator requires assignment-bound spawn and result inspection, and isolated-write for output-producing candidates. A read-only judge may return its result through the coordinator's authorized artifact capture. A cancelled result needs bound stop, terminal inspection and quarantine observations. No operation string alone establishes a method.\n\nArena adds candidates, judge, rubricArtifact and synthesis. At least two candidates share the same taskArtifact, finish before a distinct fresh judge starts and do not receive the parent rubric. Synthesis has order, inspectedCandidates, artifact and verificationArtifact; it follows completed judgment and accounts for every candidate. The review artifact assesses scoring, base selection, grafts, rejections and verification quality. File presence and phase ordering do not establish the quality of those decisions.\n\nSwarm adds partition, race, cancelled and quarantined. Partition declares requiredSlices, results mapping each slice to its assignment, and gaps. A full coverage claim requires all slices with completed inspected outputs; gaps remain explicit reduced or blocked work. Race declares rule, assignments, winner and ruleArtifact. first-pass must select the first completed survivor; rank-all needs all results; best-of retains an explicit reviewed selection. Cancelled assignments must be quarantined and cannot enter the aggregate. The combined partition-and-race case requires both methods and its cancellation exercise. The partition walkthrough exercises only its declared slices; it does not acquire an unrelated race requirement.\n\nThe three-file Swarm walkthrough requires exactly three declared slices and three completed results. Its partition-only receipt uses race null and empty cancellation, quarantine and failureExercises arrays. The combined case also records failureExercises. Each event has assignmentId, kind, attempt, order, reason and evidence, with bound result-rejection or terminal inspection. Rejected missing-revision or missing-method evidence uses attempts 0 then 1; a second invalid result exhausts the retry and leaves a failed or blocked assignment. A separate failed or blocked dropout is disclosed. These failed outputs cannot satisfy required coverage. The final assignment result metadata records the inspected terminal summary; rejection-event artifacts retain the invalid attempts.\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "input-gap-repairs/owned-safe-cleanup/outputs/source-reads/skills/technical-writing/SKILL.md",
      "bytes": 10591,
      "sha256": "409a010a405d3ea3e4c2386e2a5ce5fa7cc64dffd8304feb99282a5e099564f6",
      "owner": "local-013-input-gap-repair-owned-safe-cleanup",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "---\nname: technical-writing\ndescription: \"Layered technical-writing standard: Diátaxis structure, Google developer style sentences, STE instruction rules, Global English syntax. Use for /technical-writing or when writing or reviewing docs, RFCs, readmes, PR descriptions, or commit messages.\"\ndisable-model-invocation: true\n---\n\n# Technical writing\n\nThe goal is writing a tired engineer understands on the first read. Four layers get you there, one question each: what kind of document is this, how do sentences address the reader, how much does each sentence carry, and can any sentence be read two ways. Apply all four.\n\nThree rules sit above the layers:\n\n- **Cut every word that does no work.** If the sentence survives without a word, the word goes. \"In order to\" is \"to\". \"It is important to note that\" is nothing.\n- **Use the short, everyday word.** \"Use\", not \"utilize\". \"Help\", not \"facilitate\". \"Do\", not \"perform\". A long word has to buy its length with precision.\n- **When a rule makes a sentence worse, fix the sentence another way or leave it alone.** The rules serve the reader. A sentence that follows every rule and sounds like a machine wrote it has failed.\n\nThe codebase is the word list. Write the real symbol, file, flag, or command name, not a synonym or a description of it.\n\nDon't invent jargon. Use the words a developer would say out loud: \"move\", \"delete\", \"a budget that only decreases\", not \"evacuate\", \"ratchet\", or \"endgame\". A named pattern is fine when the doc says what it means the first time. Propose a new offender and its replacement as an addition to `unslop`'s abstract-metaphor rule in your reply, with the diff. Don't edit that skill.\n\n## Vary the rhythm\n\nThe layers decide what a document says and how much each sentence carries. A doc can obey all of them and still read machine-written: every sentence clipped short, no view anywhere, nothing specific.\n\n- Mix sentence lengths on purpose. Short sentences land a point. Longer ones that take their time carry a fact with its condition or consequence.\n- One thought per sentence does not mean one length per sentence. Split the sentence that carries two thoughts. Keep the long sentence that carries one.\n- Have a view where the mode allows it. Explanation weighs trade-offs, so say what you make of them instead of listing pros and cons. Reference stays dry.\n- Be specific over sterile. Not \"schema changes can cause issues\" but \"a column rename fails the build\".\n\n## Pick the mode first (Diátaxis)\n\nOne document, one mode. Two questions pick it: does the content inform action (doing) or understanding (thinking), and does it serve learning or work?\n\n- Action + learning: **tutorial**.\n- Action + work: **how-to**.\n- Understanding + work: **reference**.\n- Understanding + learning: **explanation**.\n\nUse the compass on a whole document or on one sentence.\n\n**Tutorial: learning by doing.** You are the teacher. The learner's success is your job, not theirs. Open by saying what the learner will build, not what they will \"learn\". Every step produces a visible result, early and often. Tell them what they should see: the expected output, the prompt change, the log line. Cut explanation to one clause and a link. Teaching pauses break the lesson. Stay concrete. Write as \"we\", in commands: \"First, do x. Now, do y.\"\n\n**How-to: steps to a goal.** Solve a problem a person has, not an operation the machine can perform. Assume competence. Skip teaching. Action only: no digressions, no background, no completeness for its own sake. Link those instead. Allow forks and judgment: \"If you want x, do y.\" Name the guide by the task: \"How to calibrate the radar array\", not \"Radar array calibration\".\n\n**Reference: facts for lookup.** Describe. Only describe. No instruction, no persuasion, no opinion. Be dry, complete, and sure. State facts, options, limits, and errors with no hedging. Mirror the structure of the thing described, so code and docs can be navigated together. Put material where readers expect it. Generate from code where possible, so it stays true.\n\n**Explanation: understanding and why.** One bounded topic, readable away from the product. Each title should tolerate an implicit \"About...\" in front. Anchor on a real why question. Give context: design decisions, history, constraints, alternatives. Opinion is allowed here and nowhere else.\n\nDon't mix modes: no reference tables inside a tutorial, no tutorial hand-holding inside reference, no arguing inside a how-to. Split and link instead.\n\n## Write sentences to the reader (Google developer style)\n\n- Talk to the reader as \"you\", in the present tense. \"Will\" only for things that genuinely happen later.\n- Say who does what: \"the compiler checks\", not \"is checked\". Passive is fine only when the actor is unknown or beside the point.\n- Write instructions as commands: \"Click Submit.\" State facts plainly. Never \"should be done\".\n- Put the condition before the instruction: \"To delete the document, click Delete.\" The reader skips what does not apply.\n- Put the common case first. Exceptions after.\n- Sound like a knowledgeable friend. No buzzwords, no figurative language, no \"please\" in instructions, and never \"simply\", \"easy\", or \"quickly\" in a procedure. If it were simple, the reader would not be here.\n- Don't pre-announce (\"we will soon support...\") and don't start consecutive sentences with the same phrase.\n- Link with words that say where the link goes: the page title or a short description. Never \"click here\". Prefer a sentence of context on the page over a link off it.\n- Headings carry the point, not just the topic (\"Pick the mode first\", not \"Modes\"). Sentence case. A task heading is a bare verb phrase (\"Create an instance\"). A concept heading is a noun phrase. One h1 per page, no skipped levels.\n- Numbered lists for sequences, bullets for everything else. Introduce a list with a complete sentence. Keep items parallel.\n- Code goes in code font. UI elements go in bold. Use serial commas. Drop \"etc.\" and say up front that a list is partial.\n\n## Make statements load one at a time (STE rules)\n\n- One instruction per sentence. One thought per sentence everywhere else.\n- Split instructions longer than about 20 words and other sentences longer than about 25.\n- Put the warning or condition before the step it guards: \"If hot oil touches your skin, injuries can occur.\"\n- Keep \"the\" and \"a\": \"Remove backup file\" reads two ways. \"Remove the backup file\" reads one.\n- Give each word one meaning and one job, then keep it. If \"check\" means inspect, don't also use it for restrain.\n- Pick one word per action and stick to it: \"start\", not \"start\" here and \"initiate\" there.\n- Write procedures as direct commands, never as narration and never in the passive: \"Install the component\", not \"the component must be installed\".\n- Avoid \"-ing\" words where you can. They take too many grammatical jobs and breed misreadings.\n\n## Leave no sentence open to two readings (Global English)\n\n- Keep words like \"only\" and \"not\" next to the word they change: \"only fails on growth\" and \"fails only on growth\" say different things.\n- Break up long noun strings: \"the proto import budget check script\" becomes \"the script that checks the proto-import budget\".\n- Make every \"it\", \"they\", and \"this\" point at one obvious thing. Repeat the noun when in doubt. Never use \"this\" or \"which\" to point at a whole clause.\n- Don't drop verbs: \"Phase 1 moves the converters and Phase 2 the runtime\" leaves Phase 2 without one. Give it one.\n- Keep the small words that show structure. \"Ensure that the switch is off\" keeps \"that\" because it makes the sentence parse one way. Never trade clarity for word count.\n- Repeat the article in a series when it prevents a misread: \"the client and the host\", not \"the client and host\", when they are two things.\n- Say which parts \"and\" or \"or\" joins when a sentence can group two ways. \"Both...and\", \"either...or\", and \"if...then\" are free disambiguators.\n- Use periods, not semicolons. Replace an em dash with a new sentence.\n- Make text in parentheses a full grammatical unit or its own sentence. Never form plurals with \"(s)\".\n- No slashes: write \"a, b, or both\" instead of \"a/b\" or \"and/or\".\n- Call each thing by one name, everywhere. A doc that says \"the gate\", \"the ratchet\", and \"the budget check\" for one thing teaches three things. Rewording an unchanged sentence between edits costs the same way. Don't churn what didn't change.\n- Skip idioms, colloquialisms, Latin abbreviations, and metaphors. A non-native reader, a translator, and an agent all parse plain constructions best.\n\n## Voice and repo specifics\n\n- Apply the **unslop** skill to every doc this skill touches. That skill owns the slop-pattern catalog: AI vocabulary, filler, hedging, formatting tells.\n- PR descriptions and commit messages are writing too. Every layer except Diátaxis applies to them. A PR body is a briefing that a reviewer can read in under a minute. Do not paste swarm logs, SHA lists, or metric tables. Link them.\n- Product UI strings are not documentation. Use your product's copy guidelines for those.\n- Indent code snippets with tabs. Write real paths and real symbols. Make every count or tree claim true at the commit that lands it, and include the command that regenerates it.\n\n## Worked example\n\nBefore:\n\n> Configuration of the proto import ratchet budget script parameters is performed via budget.json. Note that it's important to remember that running with --write, which updates the committed budget to reflect the current count, should only be done when lowering it. If exceeded, CI fails.\n\nAfter:\n\n> `budget.mjs` reads the committed budget from `budget.json` and counts the files that import protos. If the count exceeds the budget, CI fails. Run `budget.mjs --write` only to lower the budget.\n\n## Access and permissions\n\nUse the current session capabilities in `../../references/host-capabilities.md` to read documents and verify authorized edits. Supplied text is enough for a rewrite in the conversation. If document writes are unavailable or denied, return the proposed text and state that it was not saved. If the document cannot be read and no text was supplied, report the missing input. Do not invent its content. Read `../unslop/SKILL.md` before applying it. If that dependency is unavailable, report the gap and label the review partial. Preserve `disable-model-invocation` as source metadata; host enforcement needs observed evidence. These limits also apply to PR descriptions and commit messages. Sending, publishing, or creating external changes requires explicit authorization.\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "input-gap-repairs/owned-safe-cleanup/outputs/source-reads/skills/principle-encode-lessons-in-structure/SKILL.md",
      "bytes": 3348,
      "sha256": "aea2433d69ce9acefd75b825c0c60e1ee41934120c565e20046f88ab588f108d",
      "owner": "local-013-input-gap-repair-owned-safe-cleanup",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "---\nname: principle-encode-lessons-in-structure\ndescription: \"Apply when you catch yourself writing the same instruction a second time, or notice a recurring correction. Encode the rule as a lint, metadata flag, runtime check, or script instead of more text.\"\ndisable-model-invocation: true\n---\n\n# Encode Lessons in Structure\n\nEncode recurring fixes in mechanisms (tools, code, metadata, automation) instead of textual instructions. Every error, human correction, and unexpected outcome is a learning signal. Capture it, route it, and close the loop.\n\n**Why:** Textual instructions are easy to miss. They require the reader to notice, remember, and comply. Structural mechanisms (lint rules, metadata flags, runtime checks, automation scripts) enforce the rule without cooperation.\n\n**Pattern:**\nWhen you catch yourself writing the same instruction a second time:\n1. Ask: can this be a lint rule, a metadata flag, a runtime check, or a script?\n2. If yes, encode it. Delete the instruction\n3. If no (requires judgment), make the instruction more prominent and add an example of the failure mode\n\n**Pick the strongest mechanism.** When more than one mechanism would work, choose the strongest the situation allows (an unrepresentable state that cannot compile, then a lint or banned API that fails CI, then a canonical helper, then a runtime check), because agents copy whatever the surrounding code already does and a weaker guard becomes the next template.\n\n**Corollary:** If the fix is structural, only use the structural fix. The instruction is the symptom.\n\n**Feedback loop:**\n- **Capture every correction.** When the human intervenes or tests fail, decide if it's a one-off or a pattern.\n- **Route to the right layer.** One-off -> brain note. Recurring fix -> skill or lint rule. Systemic issue -> principle.\n- **Close the loop.** Don't just record. Apply now or create a concrete todo.\n\n**Anti-patterns:**\n- Acknowledging without recording (\"I'll keep that in mind\" does not persist)\n- Recording without routing (a brain note about a lint rule that should exist is wasted unless the lint rule gets implemented)\n- Fixing without generalizing (fixing one instance while leaving the recurring pattern intact)\n\n## Session capabilities and authorization\n\nFollow [session capability discovery](../../references/host-capabilities.md) for reads, writes, execution and durable persistence. Apply mechanisms within the authorized task. This principle does not authorize installation, global settings or external actions. Treat a brain note as an authorized durable record when persistence is available; claim it saved only after write and readback. If persistence is unavailable, state that limit and include the concrete todo in the current response.\n\nIf writes or execution are unavailable, provide a reduced proposal that names the check, its integration point and the remaining implementation or verification work. If the rule or source needed to select a mechanism is missing, identify the missing inputs and keep the proposal provisional. Delete a redundant instruction only once the structural fix enforces its rule. Preserve judgment-dependent guidance with a prominent failure example.\n\nPreserve explicit-invocation metadata without assuming the host enforces it. Claim enforcement, implementation or verification only for observed operations.\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "input-gap-repairs/owned-safe-cleanup/outputs/source-reads/skills/principle-guard-the-context-window/SKILL.md",
      "bytes": 1987,
      "sha256": "b0b6016d092279a26a0984adc4c601ee3c0d4e58787f4438ad9e0b5ada9e5e22",
      "owner": "local-013-input-gap-repair-owned-safe-cleanup",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "---\nname: principle-guard-the-context-window\ndescription: \"Apply when context is filling up: large outputs, long files, repeated reads, fan-out planning. Route bulk to subagents; keep summaries in the main thread, not raw payloads.\"\ndisable-model-invocation: true\n---\n\n# Guard the Context Window\n\nThe context window is finite and non-renewable within a session. Every token should be worth its cost.\n\n**Why:** Context overflow degrades reasoning quality, creates compression artifacts, and halts progress.\n\n**Pattern:**\n- **Isolate large payloads.** Route verbose outputs, screenshots, and large documents to subagents. The main context gets summaries, not raw data.\n- **Keep frequently used content inline.** Templates and references used on every invocation belong in the skill file, not in separate files that cost a read each time.\n- **Size phases and cap scope.** Limit files per phase, set turn budgets, account for mechanism costs.\n\n## Capability and permission boundaries\n\nUse the [session capability contract](../../references/host-capabilities.md) to check evidence access and independent-worker operations. Delegate only when the task authorizes it and the session exposes those operations. Follow the [assignment and integration contract](../../references/orchestration-contract.md), retain observed worker identities and inspect their results before reporting summaries. This principle grants no additional permissions.\n\nIf independent workers are unavailable or unauthorized, narrow accessible evidence into bounded sequential phases. Keep summaries in the main thread and label this reduced capability. Sequential work does not establish worker independence. If required evidence is inaccessible, name the missing input and access limit; keep the plan provisional and do not claim to have read or summarized it.\n\nPreserve the invocation metadata above without assuming the host enforces it. Report delegation and inspection only after observing the authorized operations.\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "input-gap-repairs/owned-safe-cleanup/outputs/source-reads/skills/show-me-your-work/references/decision-log-template.tsv",
      "bytes": 38,
      "sha256": "6779378006a2f5225e8c06deb84f1d913fb532041ebbb6e50d94600bfe3f4b76",
      "owner": "local-013-input-gap-repair-owned-safe-cleanup",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "ts\tphase\tdecision\twhy\tevidence\tresult\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "input-gap-repairs/owned-safe-cleanup/outputs/source-reads/skills/show-me-your-work/scripts/log.sh",
      "bytes": 1717,
      "sha256": "fb47611cbb75ea17aa80cb4f8dbfc7978b8844450b0ce702961bdef65cbb1342",
      "owner": "local-013-input-gap-repair-owned-safe-cleanup",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "#!/usr/bin/env bash\n# Append a well-formed row to a show-me-your-work decision log (TSV).\n# Usage: log.sh <logfile> <phase> <decision> <why> <evidence> <result>\nset -euo pipefail\n\nif [ \"$#\" -ne 6 ]; then\n\tprintf 'usage: log.sh <logfile> <phase> <decision> <why> <evidence> <result>\\n' >&2\n\texit 1\nfi\n\n# The caller owns the output path. Validate every argument before any write.\nif [ -z \"$1\" ]; then\n    printf 'error: an explicit owned output path is required\\n' >&2\n    exit 1\nfi\nfor value in \"$@\"; do\n    case \"$value\" in\n        *$'\\t'*|*$'\\n'*|*$'\\r'*)\n            printf 'error: tabs, newlines and carriage returns are not allowed\\n' >&2\n            exit 1\n            ;;\n    esac\ndone\nlogfile=\"$1\"\nshift\n\nlogdir=\"$(dirname \"$logfile\")\"\nif [ -n \"$logdir\" ] && [ \"$logdir\" != \".\" ] && [ ! -d \"$logdir\" ]; then\n\tmkdir -p \"$logdir\"\nfi\n\n# Use `>>` here, never `>`. A network mount can fail this test for a log\n# that exists. Then the cost is one stray header line, not the rows.\nif [ ! -s \"$logfile\" ]; then\n\tprintf 'ts\\tphase\\tdecision\\twhy\\tevidence\\tresult\\n' >> \"$logfile\"\nfi\n\nts=\"$(date -u +%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ)\"\n# Record separators were rejected above. Prefix any cell\n# whose first char a spreadsheet would parse as a formula (=, +, -, @)\n# with a single quote. The skill expects this log to be read in\n# spreadsheets, so attacker-controlled evidence (PR titles, filenames,\n# generated text) must not become formula execution when a reviewer\n# opens the file.\nclean() {\n\tlocal v\n\tv=\"$1\"\n\tcase \"$v\" in\n\t\t=*|+*|-*|@*) printf \"'%s\" \"$v\" ;;\n\t\t*) printf '%s' \"$v\" ;;\n\tesac\n}\nprintf '%s\\t%s\\t%s\\t%s\\t%s\\t%s\\n' \\\n\t\"$ts\" \"$(clean \"$1\")\" \"$(clean \"$2\")\" \"$(clean \"$3\")\" \"$(clean \"$4\")\" \"$(clean \"$5\")\" \\\n\t>> \"$logfile\"\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "input-gap-repairs/owned-safe-cleanup/outputs/source-reads/skills/show-me-your-work/SKILL.md",
      "bytes": 8456,
      "sha256": "3c4f05ffecc162b6ae376b7adcf4e7d030c8dfc65a7f26f8519f2c1544c3b98b",
      "owner": "local-013-input-gap-repair-owned-safe-cleanup",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "---\nname: show-me-your-work\ndescription: \"Keep a reviewable decision trail for long-running or unattended work: a TSV log with one row per decision (what, why, evidence, result). Local by default; commit it when a reviewer needs the trail to trust the result. Use for /show-me-your-work, autonomous or multi-phase runs, or work a human reviews after stepping away.\"\ndisable-model-invocation: true\n---\n\n# Show me your work\n\nKeep one canonical log.\n\n## The format\n\nA single TSV file, one row per decision. Cells stay single-line. Evidence is a pointer, not prose.\n\nCopy [references/decision-log-template.tsv](references/decision-log-template.tsv) (the header row) to start a clean log. Columns:\n\n- **ts.** ISO8601 timestamp.\n- **phase.** The phase or workstream.\n- **decision.** What was chosen or done, one line.\n- **why.** The reason in plain words. If a principle drove it, say it plainly, not as a jargon tag.\n- **evidence.** A link or path that proves it: commit SHA, PR number, `file:line`, or an artifact, trace, or screenshot path. Never a paragraph.\n- **result.** The outcome or predicate state: `tests green`, `reverted`, `pixel-diff 0`, `INCONCLUSIVE`, `open`.\n\nAn example, plain-spoken so a reviewer reads it at a glance.\n\n```\nts\tphase\tdecision\twhy\tevidence\tresult\n2026-05-24T09:02:00Z\tframe\tcounted the work first, about 100 components and roughly 75 hours\twanted to know the size before starting a long run\tcommit 3a9f1c2\tfound 5 things to sort out before starting\n2026-05-24T09:40:00Z\tharness\ttook screenshots of the old version before changing anything\tso we can compare old against new and catch any visual change\tscripts/snapshot.sh, baseline/\tsaved 120 reference screenshots\n2026-05-24T11:15:00Z\twidget\tmoved the widget styles over without changing how it looks\tkeep the change small and the result identical\tcommit 7c21e0a, pixel-diff 0\tlooks identical, tests pass\n2026-05-24T12:30:00Z\twidget\tthrew out a helper's work because its screenshots were blank\tchecked the real files instead of trusting its summary\tworktree reset\treverted, tightened the instructions for next time\n```\n\n## Logging a row\n\nWrite each entry the way you'd tell a teammate what you did. Plain words, concrete actions, no AI speak or abstract jargon (the **unslop** skill applies to log text too).\n\nUse the helper [scripts/log.sh](scripts/log.sh) with `<logfile> <phase> <decision> <why> <evidence> <result>` through an authorized execution binding. Pass an explicit nonempty local output path that this task owns. The helper rejects tabs, newlines and carriage returns in any argument before creating directories or writing bytes. It stamps `ts`, writes the header on first use, preserves previous rows, and prefixes any cell starting with `=`, `+`, `-`, or `@` with a single quote. Use this helper for generated or user-supplied cells so validation and formula protection apply consistently. Host execution still requires an authorized binding.\n\nLog decision points and checkpoints, not every action: a fork chosen, a unit completed with its verification result, a pivot or revert with its trigger, a blocker surfaced, a gate fixed. For loop runs, one row per iteration. Skip the trivial and self-evident.\n\nA run is one agent conversation, including its later turns and any summary of it. A pickup, a replacement agent, or a new chat starts a new run. When a run adds to a log that already has rows, its first row has phase `start`, and so does its first row after another run's `start` row. So a run that comes back to a log in a later turn first reads the log's last rows to see whether another run wrote since. A `start` row names the `ts` range of the rows before it that this run did not write, and its evidence names this run, such as its agent id. Use phase `start` for nothing else.\n\n## Where it lives\n\nBy default the log is a working artifact, not committed. Keep it at `decisions.tsv` in the work dir, or `.audit/<task-slug>.tsv` when several efforts run at once, and leave it out of git.\n\nCommit it only when the work is ambitious enough that a reviewer needs the trail to trust the result.\n\n## Rules\n\n- Append-only. A wrong call gets a new row that supersedes it. Never edit or delete history.\n- Prefer evidence produced by committed scripts over hand-made one-offs (the **encode-lessons-in-structure** principle skill).\n\n## Audit the log against the transcript\n\nAt the end of the run, before handing back, check the log told the truth. Discover this run's transcript through the current session's history binding, or an explicitly identified transcript in the active workspace. The source used `agent-transcripts/`; do not assume the host provides that directory. Never search unrelated private chats or global transcript directories. If this run's transcript is inaccessible or incomplete, record the gap and do not claim a complete transcript audit. Walk this run's rows against what actually happened. Each stretch of them begins at one of this run's `start` rows, or at the first row if this run created the log, and ends at the next `start` row of another run:\n\n- Check that every row maps to a real decision or action.\n- Check that each row's evidence resolves and shows what the row claims.\n- A fork, pivot, or abandoned approach that shaped the work but isn't logged is a gap. Add it.\n\nCorrect the log, not the story. The audit never edits or removes a row, even an invented one. When a row records neither a real decision nor a real action, or its claim or evidence is wrong, add a row that supersedes it with what actually happened and a pointer that resolves. This audit does not check rows outside this run's stretches. If this run's own work shows one of them is wrong, supersede it like any wrong call.\n\n## Cross-model review of the trail\n\nBefore handing back, use an authorized exposed independent-worker binding to request a reviewer on a different model family from the one that did the work. Follow [model selection](../../references/model-roles.md) and the [assignment contract](../../references/orchestration-contract.md). Observe the worker identity, result and actual model family before claiming independent cross-model review. If no authorized worker or verified different family is available, record that review as blocked. An authorized sequential scan is reduced capability and does not satisfy this review requirement. Self-review is not a substitute. The subagent reads the audit trail and the run's transcript, then flags what the user should pay attention to. Not a redo of the work, a scan for what's suboptimal or risky.\n\n- Decisions logged with weak or absent evidence.\n- Verification steps skipped or claimed without proof in the transcript.\n- Choices that look risky in hindsight (premature, scope-creeping, papering over a symptom).\n- Gaps the user would otherwise miss on a casual skim.\n\nEvery reply for a run that produced a trail ends with an \"Attention\" section. Lead with the reviewer's model on its own line (`reviewed by <model>`), then list each flag pointing to specific rows or moments. \"No flags\" is a valid value. The model name is not.\n\n## Reviewing the trail\n\nRead top to bottom, follow the evidence pointers, spot-check. GitHub renders a committed TSV as a table. `column -s$'\\t' -t decisions.tsv` renders it in a terminal.\n\n## Composing this skill\n\nOther skills route their audit trail here instead of inventing one. Reference it by name and let it own the format. Don't restate the columns.\n\n## Host capabilities and safety\n\nUse [session capabilities](../../references/host-capabilities.md) for history, execution, persistence and reviewer bindings. Write only to an authorized local path, then read back the saved trail before claiming persistence. A log is evidence of recorded decisions, not proof of unattended execution or recovery. Do not include credentials, tokens, raw secret-bearing output or unrelated private data. Use a safe evidence pointer or a redacted local artifact instead. Record only observed results; unresolved predicates stay `open` or `INCONCLUSIVE`.\n\nThe default local trail does not authorize a commit. Commit, publish, message or other external action requires explicit authorization. Preserve append-only history and scope every transcript audit to this run. When review is blocked, keep the Attention section and state `reviewed by unknown` if no actual reviewer model was observed, followed by the capability gap. Never use `No flags` to imply an unperformed review.\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "input-gap-repairs/owned-safe-cleanup/outputs/source-reads/skills/principle-build-the-lever/SKILL.md",
      "bytes": 3644,
      "sha256": "483e5d66b0ec7fcbf98fe7175c20d60109f6a35c3c7fc8cc34771f42fee20083",
      "owner": "local-013-input-gap-repair-owned-safe-cleanup",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "---\nname: principle-build-the-lever\ndescription: \"Apply to any non-trivial work, not just bulk work: edits, migrations, analyses, checks. Build the tool that does it or proves it (codemod, script, generator, or a skill your subagents follow) instead of working by hand. The tool is the artifact a reviewer can rerun.\"\ndisable-model-invocation: true\n---\n# Build the Lever\n\nWhen the work isn't trivial, build the tool that does it instead of doing it by hand.\n\n**Why:** Two payoffs. Throughput: a codemod, generator, or script does the work the same way every time and reruns for free. Confidence: the tool is one artifact a reviewer can read and rerun to check the work. Hand-done changes can only be re-verified by redoing them. A deterministic script turns \"trust me\" into \"run this\".\n\n**Pattern:** Default to building the lever. Skip it only when the task is trivial, a couple of obvious edits you can see at a glance.\n\n- Do the first unit by hand to learn the recipe, then build the tool. Prove it by rerunning it on that unit and diffing against your hand-done version. Make the lever safe to rerun.\n- Codemod or script for edits, generator for repetitive files, a dump-to-sqlite query for analysis, a rerunnable check for verification.\n- A deterministic lever beats fan-out. If the tool can process every unit in one pass, run it yourself. Don't fan out delegates to hand-apply what a script can do.\n- When you fan work out to subagents, write the lever as a skill they all read: the recipe, the verification contract, and the do-not-touch fences in one artifact. Keep it outside the delegates' write scope so they can't quietly edit the contract.\n- Applying this principle produces a file. If you cited it and there is no codemod, script, generator, or delegate skill in the diff, you didn't apply it.\n- Commit the lever when the work outlives the session.\n\n**Balance:** The bar is triviality, not repetition. A one-off still earns a lever when the lever is what makes the work checkable. Per the [Laziness Protocol](../principle-laziness-protocol/SKILL.md), build the smallest script that does or proves the job, never a framework.\n\nDistinct from [Encode Lessons in Structure](../principle-encode-lessons-in-structure/SKILL.md), which makes a recurring instruction a durable guardrail. This is throughput and reviewability on the work in front of you. For scripting the verification itself, see [Prove It Works](../principle-prove-it-works/SKILL.md).\n\n## Host capability and permission boundaries\n\nUse the [host capability contract](../../references/host-capabilities.md) to discover file reads, authorized writes, execution and persistence. A proposed tool is not a saved artifact. A saved artifact is not executed proof. Report the commands, exit status and comparisons you observed; if execution is denied or unavailable, state that proof is blocked and provide the proposed rerun and diff procedure as reduced capability. If source reads are unavailable, do not claim a verified recipe.\n\nDelegation requires user authorization and exposed independent-worker operations under the [orchestration contract](../../references/orchestration-contract.md). Keep the shared recipe outside worker write scopes. Sequential work is reduced capability and never proves independent workers. Preserve the [active workflow owner](../../references/workflow-ownership.md).\n\nRetaining the lever beyond the session does not grant commit permission. Commit only when authorized. Do not install dependencies, publish or perform external writes as incidental steps. Preserve `disable-model-invocation: true` metadata without assuming the host enforces it.\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "input-gap-repairs/owned-safe-cleanup/outputs/source-reads/skills/poteto-mode/playbooks/worktree-cleanup.md",
      "bytes": 6998,
      "sha256": "610f822cc78a222f6cf8c6cf980436e36ff58b72c820b36c840461375bb8ed10",
      "owner": "local-013-input-gap-repair-owned-safe-cleanup",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "### Worktree and simulator cleanup\n\nReclaim disk space by pruning merged or abandoned Git worktrees and stale iOS simulators. You own the audit and safety gate. Deletion can destroy uncommitted or ignored work, so preserve anything in use, unrelated to this request, or lacking evidence. Follow [Autonomy](../SKILL.md), [session capabilities](../../../references/host-capabilities.md) and the [workflow owner](../../../references/workflow-ownership.md). No audit bucket grants permission to delete user state.\n\n1. Snapshot and audit. Record `df -h /`, then use the existing [read-only audit wrapper](../scripts/worktree-audit.sh) and its [implementation](../scripts/worktree-audit.py), as required by [Build the lever](../../principle-build-the-lever/SKILL.md). Run through Bun without installing dependencies, from the package root:\n\n   ```sh\n   bun --no-install -e 'const p=Bun.spawnSync([\"bash\",\"skills/poteto-mode/scripts/worktree-audit.sh\",...process.argv.slice(1)]);process.stdout.write(p.stdout);process.stderr.write(p.stderr);process.exit(p.exitCode)' -- --repo <repository-path> --base <verified-local-base-ref> --history-map <authorized-history-export.json> --pr-state <authorized-pr-export.json>\n   ```\n\n   Paths come from `git worktree list --porcelain -z`, never hand-typed, under [Encode lessons in structure](../../principle-encode-lessons-in-structure/SKILL.md). The helper excludes the primary checkout, returns JSON sorted by logical size in bytes, and reports HEAD age in days, merge state, dirty state, local tracking state, PR records, last-chat timestamp and a suggested bucket. It performs no fetch, forge request, private transcript discovery or deletion. Supply an explicit verified local base. Tracking refs and PR exports may be stale; record their freshness before treating them as evidence. A CLOSED PR is not merge evidence. Ancestry and an explicitly MERGED PR account for different merge methods.\n\n   The optional history export maps complete paths to past Unix timestamps, or null for explicitly known absent history. A missing key is unknown. PR input is an array of `number`, `state` and `headRefName` records; omitted input is unknown, while an explicit empty array says there are no matching exported PRs. Null age, remote, dirty or other evidence means hold, never permission. Logical size is not allocated disk usage. Ignored files are outside Git dirty counts. Retain the raw audit as an artifact. Background a slow authorized history scan only when the host exposes and successfully runs that operation; otherwise disclose sequential reduced capability.\n\n2. The bucket is advice. The pinned and active chats are the real usage artifact under [Prove it works](../../principle-prove-it-works/SKILL.md). Get that set from the user or an exposed, authorized sidebar/history operation and cross-check every candidate. A `review-merged` row still needs this check. Preserve the primary and current checkout, unrelated worktrees, and every pinned or active chat's worktree. Missing sidebar, history, ownership or freshness evidence holds the candidate.\n\n3. Verify usage before deleting. For `verify-recent-chat` rows and anything in doubt, use authorized read-only workers to inspect the relevant accessible transcripts and report pinned or ongoing chats and every worktree they touch. Follow [Guard the context window](../../principle-guard-the-context-window/SKILL.md), the [poteto prompt](../../../references/agent-prompts/poteto-agent.md), [model roles](../../../references/model-roles.md) and the [assignment contract](../../../references/orchestration-contract.md). Retain worker identities, revision and inspected results. Pinned chats may use sibling arena or reproduction worktrees absent from the sidebar. Without authorized independent workers, inspect bounded evidence sequentially and label reduced capability. Without the needed transcripts or user confirmation, hold the path. An export's last timestamp alone does not prove absence of live usage.\n\n4. Pause on irreversible loss. `wip:N` counts tracked changes; show the diff and get a decision. `scratch:N` counts untracked files, not proof they are disposable. Name those files and obtain a decision before discarding them. Inspect ignored build artifacts and other ignored user state separately. Unknown dirty state, age or tracking evidence holds the path. Clean, merged and not-in-use paths are candidates only within the recorded cleanup scope and after the applicable user deletion gate. Record each exact Git-listed path, HEAD, usage evidence, merge evidence, files at risk, intended action and explicit authorization. Upstream autonomy does not waive this gate.\n\n5. Prune only the confirmed set. Re-list worktrees and recheck HEAD, status, ignored state and usage immediately before removal. A changed path, new usage or unknown outcome blocks the action. For an authorized exact path, `git worktree remove --force <path>` is the source removal operation. If ignored artifacts leave the directory behind, inspect the remainder and obtain authorization for that exact directory before `rm -rf <path>`. Never widen the path or delete an unrelated directory. Run `git worktree prune` only within the authorized repository cleanup. Branch refs survive normal worktree removal, but that does not recover discarded uncommitted or ignored files. Confirm each result with a fresh list and `df -h /`; after interruption inspect the recorded action and destination before any retry.\n\n6. Simulators and other reclaimers. Discover local macOS/Xcode tools and identify in-use devices first. The source operations are `xcrun simctl --set testing delete all` for XCTestDevices clones, `xcrun simctl delete unavailable`, and `xcrun simctl runtime list` followed by `xcrun simctl runtime delete <id>` for an approved old runtime. These delete user state. Show the exact targets and get the applicable deletion authorization before execution. If tools are absent or execution is denied, report this portion blocked. Other candidates include Xcode DerivedData and iOS DeviceSupport, the source editor's application-support database backups and workspace snapshots, and pnpm, uv, brew or yarn caches. Discover actual host paths rather than assume a source-editor data location. Clear only named caches within authorization that the user has not said to keep. Do not infer abandonment from size or age.\n\nThe gates above are the review for this playbook. If deletion is unauthorized, produce the audit and held-candidate report as reduced capability. If even the required reads or execution are unavailable, report the missing binding and blocked audit. Never claim reclaimed space from proposed commands.\n\nReply with `df -h /` before and after, measured space reclaimed, worktrees actually pruned, and a one-line reason for each held path, including the chat using it or the files at risk. Keep unavailable measurements explicit. If nothing was removed, say so and distinguish the audit from completed cleanup.\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "input-gap-repairs/owned-safe-cleanup/outputs/source-reads/skills/poteto-mode/scripts/worktree-audit.py",
      "bytes": 6770,
      "sha256": "0e26a1aced5f54cc562abda097017a7020ac8bfeb82307bc6666236b20a4be79",
      "owner": "local-013-input-gap-repair-owned-safe-cleanup",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "\"\"\"Read-only worktree audit adapted from Lauren Tan's MIT-licensed pstack.\n\nCLI exports: history-map is an object keyed by complete worktree paths, with\nUnix last-chat timestamps or null for explicitly known absent history.\npr-state is an array of {number, state, headRefName} records. Neither input\nis discovered automatically. Null output fields mean unavailable evidence.\nThe first worktree is retained as the primary checkout and excluded, as upstream.\n\"\"\"\nimport argparse\nimport json\nimport os\nfrom pathlib import Path\nimport subprocess\nimport time\n\n\ndef _git(repo, *args):\n    return subprocess.run(['git', '-C', str(repo), *args], capture_output=True,\n                          env={**os.environ, 'GIT_OPTIONAL_LOCKS': '0', 'GIT_NO_LAZY_FETCH': '1'})\n\n\ndef _text(result):\n    return os.fsdecode(result.stdout).strip() if result.returncode == 0 else None\n\n\ndef _size(path):\n    try:\n        total = 0\n        def fail(error):\n            raise error\n        for root, dirs, files in os.walk(path, followlinks=False, onerror=fail):\n            for name in files:\n                total += (Path(root) / name).lstat().st_size\n        return total if path.is_dir() else None\n    except OSError:\n        return None\n\n\ndef audit_worktrees(repo: Path, base: str, history_map: dict | None, pr_state: list | None) -> list[dict]:\n    if not base or base.startswith('-'):\n        raise ValueError('base must be an explicit Git ref')\n    if history_map is not None:\n        if not isinstance(history_map, dict) or any(not isinstance(k, str) or (v is not None and (type(v) not in (int, float) or not 0 <= v <= time.time())) for k, v in history_map.items()):\n            raise ValueError('history-map must map complete paths to past Unix timestamps or null')\n    if pr_state is not None:\n        if not isinstance(pr_state, list) or any(not isinstance(p, dict) or not isinstance(p.get('headRefName'), str) or type(p.get('number')) is not int or p['number'] <= 0 or p.get('state') not in ('OPEN', 'CLOSED', 'MERGED') for p in pr_state):\n            raise ValueError('pr-state must contain number, state and headRefName records')\n    listing = _git(repo, 'worktree', 'list', '--porcelain', '-z')\n    if listing.returncode:\n        raise ValueError('cannot list local worktrees: ' + os.fsdecode(listing.stderr).strip())\n    records = []\n    current = {}\n    for token in os.fsdecode(listing.stdout).split('\\0'):\n        if not token:\n            if current:\n                records.append(current)\n                current = {}\n        else:\n            key, _, value = token.partition(' ')\n            current[key] = value\n    if current:\n        records.append(current)\n    base_head = _text(_git(repo, 'rev-parse', '--verify', base + '^{commit}'))\n    rows = []\n    now = time.time()\n    for record in records[1:]:\n        path = Path(record['worktree'])\n        head = _text(_git(path, 'rev-parse', '--verify', 'HEAD^{commit}'))\n        branch = _text(_git(path, 'symbolic-ref', '--quiet', '--short', 'HEAD'))\n        stamp = _text(_git(path, 'log', '-1', '--format=%ct', 'HEAD'))\n        age = max(0, int((now - int(stamp)) // 86400)) if stamp and stamp.isdigit() else None\n        merged = None\n        if head and base_head:\n            check = _git(repo, 'merge-base', '--is-ancestor', head, base_head)\n            merged = True if check.returncode == 0 else False if check.returncode == 1 else None\n        status = _git(path, 'status', '--porcelain=v1', '-z', '--untracked-files=all')\n        tracked = untracked = 0\n        entries = status.stdout.split(b'\\0')\n        i = 0\n        while i < len(entries):\n            item = entries[i]\n            if item:\n                if item[:2] == b'??':\n                    untracked += 1\n                else:\n                    tracked += 1\n                    if b'R' in item[:2] or b'C' in item[:2]:\n                        i += 1\n            i += 1\n        dirty = None if status.returncode else f'wip:{tracked}' if tracked else f'scratch:{untracked}' if untracked else 'clean'\n        remote = None\n        if head and branch is None:\n            remote = 'detached'\n        elif branch and head:\n            # Use the configured local tracking ref, never assume origin.\n            upstream = _text(_git(path, 'rev-parse', '--verify', '@{upstream}^{commit}'))\n            if upstream:\n                ahead = _text(_git(path, 'rev-list', '--count', upstream + '..' + head))\n                remote = 'pushed' if upstream == head else 'ahead' + ahead if ahead else None\n            else:\n                remote = 'no-remote'\n        matches = None if pr_state is None or branch is None else [p for p in pr_state if p['headRefName'] == branch]\n        last = history_map.get(str(path)) if history_map is not None else None\n        history_known = history_map is not None and str(path) in history_map\n        recent = last is not None and (now - last) / 86400 <= 4\n        size = _size(path)\n        if dirty is not None and dirty != 'clean':\n            bucket = 'hold-wip'\n        elif matches and any(p['state'] == 'OPEN' for p in matches):\n            bucket = 'hold-open-pr'\n        elif dirty is None or merged is None or matches is None or not history_known or size is None or age is None or remote is None:\n            bucket = 'hold-unknown'\n        elif recent:\n            bucket = 'verify-recent-chat'\n        elif merged or any(p['state'] == 'MERGED' for p in matches):\n            bucket = 'review-merged'\n        else:\n            bucket = 'review'\n        rows.append(dict(size=size, age=age, merged=merged, dirty=dirty, remote=remote,\n                         pr=matches, last_chat=last, bucket=bucket, worktree=str(path),\n                         head=head, branch=branch, tracked_changes=tracked if dirty is not None else None,\n                         untracked_changes=untracked if dirty is not None else None,\n                         history_known=history_known, base=base, base_head=base_head))\n    return sorted(rows, key=lambda row: row['size'] if row['size'] is not None else -1, reverse=True)\n\n\ndef main():\n    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__)\n    parser.add_argument('--repo', required=True, type=Path)\n    parser.add_argument('--base', required=True)\n    parser.add_argument('--history-map', type=Path)\n    parser.add_argument('--pr-state', type=Path)\n    args = parser.parse_args()\n    try:\n        history = json.loads(args.history_map.read_text()) if args.history_map else None\n        prs = json.loads(args.pr_state.read_text()) if args.pr_state else None\n        print(json.dumps(audit_worktrees(args.repo, args.base, history, prs), ensure_ascii=True))\n    except (ValueError, OSError) as error:\n        parser.error(str(error))\n\n\nif __name__ == '__main__':\n    main()\n"
    },

      "text": "#!/usr/bin/env bash\n# Read-only audit. Explicit local base and optional user-scoped JSON exports.\nset -euo pipefail\nexec python3 -B \"$(cd -- \"$(dirname -- \"${BASH_SOURCE[0]}\")\" && pwd)/worktree-audit.py\" \"$@\"\n"

      "path": "input-gap-repairs/owned-safe-cleanup/outputs/source-reads/skills/poteto-mode/SKILL.md",
